# 16: The constants of rung 0, and what would bind each one

Type: research
Status: resolved
Claimed by: r05-list
Blocked by: 02, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15

**Question:** For each constant a rung 0 law reads, what measurable quantity would bind it? How does that quantity map to the parameter, is the form fitted at the instant where its source used a mean, and which works on hand already name it?

This is the first part of [Parameters](05-parameters.md). Ticket 05 binds the values, system by system, and starts from this list. The list binds no value, and no row enters `data/`.

## Answer

Every constant of every rung 0 law has a row below, 556 in all. The laws are the slice's (research 02, the crates' parameter keys and the native-contract tickets that state a slice law), the forms of research 11 to 15, and the receptor binding each of those forms reads. [Body ticket 01](../../body/issues/01-rung-0-specification.md)'s Answer decides which laws rung 0 keeps. A constant of a law it does not keep has no row.

### How to read the list

- **A row** is one constant at its binding level. It binds at the site-class default unless a sourced check already earned a split (record 0002), so a constant per bed class is one row. A constant that differs by site, such as a valve's area or a vessel segment's elastance, has one row for each site the slice binds.
- **The ID**, such as `CV-041`, is the system and a number. `grep '^| CV-041 '` finds the row. The IDs hold only inside this file.
- **The system** is the one whose law reads the constant, by body 01. The receptor layer's binding rows sit under NS for the transmitters and under the hormone's system for a hormone.
- **The route** says how the value can enter the packet:
  - `sourced`: a quantity measured in people binds it, at whichever tier is on hand (SRS section 6);
  - `derivation`: it follows from other rows by the algebra in its mapping;
  - `inference`: no method measures it, so an inference record fills it (records 0003 and 0004), and the row says what the record would reason from;
  - `model default`: a structural or numerical choice the forms make, which no study measures;
  - `unknown`: the row says why the route cannot be told yet.
- **The mapping** takes the measured quantity to the parameter, with units, and names every other row it reads. "No row holds it" marks a quantity the mapping needs that no row of this list holds, such as an organ's mass.
- **At the instant** is "yes" where the engine evaluates the form at the instant and its source model used a mean (ticket 05's last Comment, from body ticket 04). Such a constant is fitted against the form as the engine evaluates it.
- **On hand** lists the works the law-form tickets, ticket 05's Comments and the curation study already name for the row, by their number in the works table, each with its locator, its tier and who named it. Tier `unknown` marks a fitted model, a model report or a theory paper: SRS section 6 has no tier for a model's fitted value, so the appraiser sets one. "None named" leaves the search to the worker who binds that system.
- **The flags** after each system's table are the hard parts the rows found. The sections below gather them.

### Reading order

One document answers many rows, so open the works in the order of the first table: each is ranked by how many rows it could answer, and it lists them. Three textbooks lead it (W001 to W003). They answer many rows at the textbook tier, so they give a first value fast, and a primary work replaces each where one is on hand. Several of the next works are fitted models (tier `unknown`), whose values are fits, not measurements. They show a form's scale and give a check, and a primary measurement binds the value where one exists.

Then bind the systems in the order the ticket's boxes give: CV, FL, RN, NS and the receptor layer, then RS, BC, EN, HP, GI, HC, MT, MS and IM. The second table gives each system's rows and the works that answer most of them.

141 rows carry a key a crate reads today, and 415 have no key yet. 27 rows are flagged as fitted at the instant where their source model used a mean: CV-083, CV-084, RN-040, RN-041, RN-042, RN-053, RN-054, NS-017, NS-018, NS-020, NS-021, NS-022, NS-023, NS-028, NS-029, NS-031, NS-032, NS-034, NS-035, NS-036, EN-028, EN-029, GI-002, GI-003, GI-033, GI-034, GI-035. 115 rows have no work named for them. 168 rows read a quantity no row holds. The works table holds 280 works.

### What cannot be bound

No row has the route "none". Record 0003 lets an inference record fill any parameter that no source covers, so every constant that is a parameter has a route. What cannot be bound falls in four groups. Each is a finding for this ticket and for the ticket that owns the form, not a gap for the next worker to fill.

**A produced quantity written as a constant.** SRS invariant I14 says a produced quantity is never a parameter, so no route binds these as their forms write them. The form must read the produced quantity, or a declared reference that is not the body's own value.

- RS-011, `F` of the alveolar gas equation, follows from the alveolar PCO2, the inspired oxygen fraction and the exchange ratio.
- MT-005, `RQ`, is a constant only until ticket 16. From then on research 13's fuel mix makes it follow what is burned.
- HC-002, `k_R` at a site, is scaled by the gut blood's "fixed share" of the red cell mass, which the body produces.
- EN-011, `G_b` of glucagon secretion, normalizes by basal glucose. Only `G_b x_50` sets the curve, so it binds as a declared reference and never as the body's fasting glucose.
- NS-061, `V_ref`, and RN-054, `q_ref`, bind only as declared references. The engine's own resting central volume or macula densa flow would be a produced quantity.
- RS-010, the alveolar water vapour pressure, is a produced quantity if research 12's saturated vapour pressure is read at core temperature.
- BC-014, `W_pl`, follows plasma protein, which is an amount at rung 0.
- CV-016, CV-017 and CV-039 divide by a resting receptor effect. That effect is produced, so they bind only from the engine's resting effect, after the receptor rows.

**Constants that no measurement separates.** Only a combination of each set enters the form or the data. One constant of each set is a unit choice or a model default, and the ticket owes a ruling on which.

- EN-021 `R0` and EN-025 `dK` of aldosterone enter only as `R0 / dK`. EN-032 `R1` and NS-058 `dP` of vasopressin enter only as `R1 / dP`.
- NS-057 `θ0`, NS-060 `k_V` and NS-061 `V_ref` enter only as `θ0 - k_V V_ref`.
- RN-049 `J1`, RN-051 `c_ref`, RN-053 `S_q` and RN-054 `q_ref` of potassium secretion absorb one another.
- BC-034 `a_Hb` trades off with the lumped anion's amount at admission.
- HP-002 to HP-006: each liver crossing `P_X` sits in series with its reaction (HP-016, HP-018, HP-020, HP-022), so splanchnic uptake binds only the pair. MT-063 is the same for glycerol in the other beds.
- HP-007 `k_syn` and HP-011 `k_brk`: spectroscopy of the liver sees only the net change in glycogen.
- MT-016 to MT-019: each heat store's reference temperature and heat.
- MT-030 and MT-031 of the vapour pressure form enter only as `P_ref exp(a)`.
- MT-002 `PCO2_ref` and MT-003 `N_ref` enter only as `PCO2_ref - N_ref / K_t`.
- CV-031 `a_peak` and CV-012 to CV-015 `E_active`.
- The thermostat's commands, NS-037 to NS-045. Data bind only the product of each command's slope, the junction's release per traffic, `eps` and the effector's maximum. Only thresholds are measurable, so the target's mean (NS-047), the three thresholds and the skin reference need one convention.

**A constant the contract cannot state yet.** These are measurable, but no parameter can hold them until the contract grows. Body tickets 03 to 05, and the tickets the flags name, own the change.

- Heat has no identity rows, so a heat capacity, a store's temperature and a conductance times a temperature difference have no slot: MT-013 to MT-020 and CV-082.
- The body's surface and clothing's insulation have no dimension (MT-021 to MT-023), there is no speed (MT-024), and no row takes a temperature over a temperature (MT-032 and MT-033).
- No row takes a flow over a flow (RN-054), or an osmolality per volume times a volume (NS-060).
- Binding at a junction reads the cleft's amount, so every junction's on rate folds in a cleft volume, which no row and no parameter holds.
- Insulin acts through each bed's interstitium (MT-051, MT-055), but the receptor layer binds only in a cleft or in plasma.
- The skin's active vasodilation names no receptor, so CV-083, CV-086 and CV-087 have no assay until it does.

**A rung 0 law whose form names no constant.** These laws are kept and no ticket states their form, so nothing yet exists to bind. Each needs a form before its constants can have rows.

- Oxygen and carbon dioxide across the capillary wall and the cell membrane (body 01 MT, line 499).
- Lactate's oxidation and clearance.
- Lung stretch to NS (arrow 83), and the work of breathing and the pump's load (arrows 84 and 85).
- The dry heat of warming inspired gas. Research 12's Kingma supplement may hold it.
- The breathing rhythm: how drive splits into rate and depth.

Three laws name no constant by design and need none: the liver's glucose output as a check, the kidney's creatinine as filtration times concentration, and ketones into the cells, which reuses the liver's `P_ket`. Immunity and coagulation are declared with zero flux at rung 0 (the body map's default), so IM and coagulation have no row.

### Hard parts

**A constant two tickets define differently.** Each needs one definition before its value is bound.

- Vasopressin. The slice's held law is a logistic of central venous sodium with a maximum rate (EN-001 to EN-003). Research 14 writes an osmolality threshold with a linear rise and no maximum (NS-057 to NS-061, EN-032).
- Sodium reclaim. Research 14's `J0` (RN-039) is the capacity with every effect at zero, while the slice's `rn.sodium-capacity` (RN-016, RN-018) holds at rest.
- The macula densa. Research 14's renin law sets a second set point, `x0` (RN-033), on the sodium that the slice's `rn.feedback-sodium` already reads.
- `K1` (BC-018). Research 11 takes Dash's pH and temperature terms, and research 14 takes Antonius's `Kc` as one constant whose printed unit does not fit its equation.
- Water's latent heat. Research 11 writes it as a fixed number inside its airway form (RS-015), and research 12 makes it a constant to bind (MT-028). One record should serve both.
- The skin's constrictor (CV-085). Research 02 reads alpha-1, and research 12 writes "alpha" and cites alpha-2 in the cold.
- Insulin at the fat bed. Research 13 gives lipolysis a second `eps` (MT-061), while research 02 keys `eps` by receptor, ligand and tissue.
- Insulin's efficacy at the liver. Research 13 uses one `eps` (HP-010), and research 15 makes it `eps_I0 / (1 + S_F L)` (HP-030). A healthy daytime measurement gives only their value at the day's cortisol.
- The fecal exit. Research 13's exit (GI-020 to GI-026) fixes the share absorbed, so research 15's saturable calcium and magnesium absorption and iron's store factor (GI-029 to GI-036) set only how fast the lumen empties. Data on the fraction absorbed bind research 13's `f_i`, and only rate data bind research 15's constants.
- Temperature. Research 11's Decisions keep a temperature shift on P50, while body 01 holds every speciation at a reference temperature. The rows follow body 01, and the temperature terms have no row.
- One constant, two names or two keys. The sinus node's `R0` is `ns.intrinsic_rate.sinoatrial` in research 02 and `data/`, and `cv.intrinsic-rate` in the crate (CV-036). `cv.albumin-volume` and `fl.albumin-volume` are one constant (CV-061, FL-008), and so are `fl.oncotic-slope` and `rn.oncotic-slope` (FL-002, RN-006). Hemoglobin's partial volume and the red cells' volume could become two records of one quantity (HC-001).
- Fixed numbers held as packet parameters. Research 02's forms fix `rn.feedback-rest` (RN-021) at 1 and `rn.feedback-middle` (RN-022) at one half. `cv.half` (CV-062) is one half, `cv.cycle-end` (CV-035) is one beat, and `cv.feedback-share` (CV-079) is anatomy. Each is a model default, and none is a clinical value.

**A value bound twice.** Native-contract 10 fits the renal afferent resistance (CV-066) and the tubule's sodium capacity (RN-016) to anchors at initialization, so a sourced value would bind each one a second time. Blood density reaches three crate keys folded into each (CV-053), so the three can disagree on it.

**A binding that spends a check.** These constants bind from the same kind of measurement that the curation study lists as an anchor or a validation, so binding them makes that check partly circular: `D_bed` (MT-004), `V_D` (RS-013), `V_L0`, `V_W0` and `dV_W` against the functional residual capacity (RS-003, RS-005, RS-006), `K_t` (MT-001), the chemoreflex gains (NS-028, NS-031), the drive wakefulness adds (NS-034), the set point's rhythm (NS-047 to NS-052), cortisol's `S_0` (EN-040), the red cells' `k_R` (HC-002), and `pH0` taken from the arterial pH anchor (RN-063). `S_P` (RN-040) must never be fitted to the pressure natriuresis check it is meant to pass.

**A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds.** The counts give how many rows read one. Most are masses or volumes: each bed's tissue and water, the liver, muscle and fat, each junction's cleft and tissue, the vessel segments' blood, the nephron's geometry, the intestine's absorbing length, and the body's surface for a value given per 1.73 m². The others are shares and ratios: the regional to whole-body sweat ratio, each NREM stage's share of the night, the ultrafilterable share of magnesium, cortisol's free fraction, and the central veins' share where PTH and cortisol clear. Some may be anchors or inputs of the reference man; the rest need rows of their own.

**Receptor binding.** No law-form ticket wrote the binding constants of the receptors its new `eps` rows read. The list adds them under the receptor layer's form, routed sourced from in vitro binding of the human receptor: insulin (HP-038, MT-077, MT-078, EN-044, EN-045), glucagon (HP-039, EN-046, EN-047), AT1 (RN-082, RN-083, EN-048 to EN-050), the mineralocorticoid receptor (RN-084, EN-051, EN-052), the natriuretic peptide receptor (RN-085, EN-053, EN-054), M3 at the sweat glands (MT-079 to MT-081), the nicotinic receptor at the respiratory pump (MS-021 to MS-023), and the skin's unnamed vasodilator receptor (CV-087). Research 15 routes its own receptor sites, PTH1R and the glucocorticoid receptor, to an inference record, so one convention is owed.

**What the crates read today.** Every keyed row comes from the slice. Seven slice constants have no key yet: Katona's `m` and `n` (in `data/`), blood density, plasma viscosity and the viscosity diameter, and native-contract 33's collapse share and exponent.

### The rows, system by system

Columns: the parameter (its constant, key and binding level); the law, the ticket row that states its form, and the body 01 row that keeps it; the measurable quantity and the route; the mapping with units and the rows it reads; whether the form is fitted at the instant where its source used a mean; and the works on hand, by their number in the works table. Each system's flags follow its table.

Flags for CV:

- CV-004: The wall law is linear in stressed volume, so one E stands for a curved EDPVR or venous pressure-volume curve only near the operating point; 'p = E s' in walls.rs
- CV-007: Ticket 05's pilot shows arterial compliance depends on the pressure it is read at (Stergiopulos 1999), while the slice's wall law holds one linear E.
- CV-009: The mapping needs the legs' tissue volume, which no row holds; ticket 05's pilot says calf per 100 mL reaches the whole leg only by a justified scaling.
- CV-009: The wall law is linear in stressed volume, so one E stands for a curved EDPVR or venous pressure-volume curve only near the operating point; 'p = E s' in walls.rs
- CV-010: The measured volume change mixes the caval veins with the right atrium unless the method images the veins alone.
- CV-011: The derivation needs total vascular compliance and each bed's share of microvascular volume, which no row holds.
- CV-011: A bed's capillary compliance is a difference of large numbers; a small error in the segment rows moves it far.
- CV-011: Total systemic compliance covers the systemic beds only; the pulmonary bed's compliance needs its own measure (pulmonary blood volume against pulmonary pressures).
- CV-012: `cv.activation-peak` and `cv.active-elastance` enter the wall law only as their product, so the pair is not separately identifiable from a pressure-volume loop.
- CV-013: `cv.activation-peak` and `cv.active-elastance` enter the wall law only as their product, so the pair is not separately identifiable from a pressure-volume loop.
- CV-014: The slice gives every chamber one activation state, so an atrium's active elastance would contract with the ventricles, not before them; body 01 asks for four time-varying chambers ('rhythm.rs' declares one 'activation'). The slice binds the atria's active elastance at zero.
- CV-015: The slice gives every chamber one activation state, so an atrium's active elastance would contract with the ventricles, not before them; body 01 asks for four time-varying chambers ('rhythm.rs' declares one 'activation'). The slice binds the atria's active elastance at zero.
- CV-016: A derivation that divides by a resting receptor effect reads a produced quantity at rest; it must use the engine's resting effect, never a parameter (SRS I14).
- CV-017: A derivation that divides by a resting receptor effect reads a produced quantity at rest; it must use the engine's resting effect, never a parameter (SRS I14).
- CV-018: The wall law uses one V_u for the passive and the active elastance, while the ESPVR's V0 and the EDPVR's volume at zero pressure differ; 'stressed' in walls.rs subtracts one V_u from both.
- CV-020: The mapping needs the right atrium' resting blood volume, which no row holds (SUB-P03 gives total blood volume, not its split by segment).
- CV-021: The mapping needs the left atrium' resting blood volume, which no row holds (SUB-P03 gives total blood volume, not its split by segment).

- CV-025: The mapping needs the splanchnic veins' resting blood volume, which no row holds (SUB-P03 gives total blood volume, not its split by segment).

- CV-027: The mapping needs the central veins' resting blood volume, which no row holds (SUB-P03 gives total blood volume, not its split by segment).

- CV-029: The measured shift is in tone-moved volume, and the effect it rode on is not measured, so S binds only with a model of the challenge's occupancy (ticket 05's pilot: 'no measure of the effect the shift rode on').
- CV-030: The measured shift is in tone-moved volume, and the effect it rode on is not measured, so S binds only with a model of the challenge's occupancy (ticket 05's pilot: 'no measure of the effect the shift rode on').
- CV-031: `cv.activation-peak` is a scale with no physical meaning apart from `cv.active-elastance`; binding it as a measured value would double-count Ees.
- CV-032: A first-order rise never peaks inside systole, so the measured time to peak maps to k_rise only through a chosen share of a_peak; the form is a model default.
- CV-034: The form ends systole at a fixed share of the cycle, while measured systole shortens less than diastole as the rate rises, so the share holds only near the resting rate.
- CV-035: A unit factor of the form is read as a packet parameter, so a packet could set a cycle that is not one beat and the heart rate would no longer be the rate the phase runs at.
- CV-036: Research 02 names this constant `ns.intrinsic_rate.sinoatrial` and data/ holds it under that id, while the crate reads `cv.intrinsic-rate`; one constant, two names.
- CV-036: Katona's abstract names no posture; XC-36 only assumes its rest matches the supine anchor (ticket 05 Comment).
- CV-037: `m` is a packet record no crate reads; it reaches the engine only through `cv.beta-gain` = (m - 1) / E_b1.
- CV-038: `n` is a packet record no crate reads; it reaches the engine only through `cv.vagal-gain` = (1 - n) / E_m2.

Flags for FL:

- FL-001: An extensive constant bound at the bed-class default needs a per-bed tissue scale, which no row holds; slice.rs binds it per bed.
- FL-001: Limb CFC binds the leg bed; the splanchnic, pulmonary and renal beds have no human method named, so a class default carries the limb's value to them.
- FL-002: `fl.oncotic-slope` and `rn.oncotic-slope` state one physical relation under two keys.
- FL-002: Plasma COP comes from albumin and globulins, while the law carries albumin alone, so the slope fitted to plasma COP over albumin folds globulins in; Landis-Pappenheimer is not linear.
- FL-003: slice.rs gives the peritubular bed its own reflection, a split no sourced check has earned (record 0002).
- FL-003: The named primary is in rats.
- FL-004: An extensive constant bound at the bed-class default needs a per-bed tissue scale, which no row holds; slice.rs binds it per bed.
- FL-004: One g serves sodium, potassium and chloride, which have different diffusion coefficients.
- FL-005: Whole-body TER binds a sum over beds, so the per-bed split is a model default unless a bed-level measure is found.
- FL-005: An extensive constant bound at the bed-class default needs a per-bed tissue scale, which no row holds; slice.rs binds it per bed.
- FL-006: An extensive constant bound at the bed-class default needs a per-bed tissue scale, which no row holds; slice.rs binds it per bed.
- FL-006: Interstitial compliance is strongly nonlinear above zero pressure, and the slice holds it linear (native-contract 10 'A nonlinear interstitial compliance').
- FL-007: The derivation needs each bed's interstitial volume and pressure, which no row holds.
- FL-008: `fl.albumin-volume` and `cv.albumin-volume` state one physical constant under two keys.
- FL-009: An extensive constant bound at the bed-class default needs a per-bed tissue scale, which no row holds; slice.rs binds it per bed.
- FL-009: slice.rs gives the glomerular and peritubular lymph edges their own conductance, a split no sourced check has earned (record 0002).
- FL-010: The lymph flow curve the textbooks show is canine.
- FL-013: Two-pool urea kinetics are measured mostly in dialysis patients, not healthy adults; urea's volume of distribution equal to total body water (Tietz) says only that the crossing is fast
- FL-013: Splitting one whole-body exchange clearance across beds needs each bed's cell water, which no row holds
- FL-014: Waikar and Bonventre's exchange clearance is a model input, not a measurement; splitting it across beds needs each bed's cell water, which no row holds
- FL-015: Clausen's pump capacity is the maximum with intracellular sodium saturated, while this form saturates only in interstitial potassium and has no intracellular sodium term, so the capacity is not this V_max
- FL-015: The mapping needs each bed's cell mass, which no row holds
- FL-017: The mapping needs intracellular potassium at rest, which no row holds
- FL-018: The mapping needs intracellular sodium at rest, which no row holds
- FL-019: Milo and Phillips say the energy depends on the cell's conditions, so one body-wide value stands for all beds
- FL-020: Chien and Macey measure diffusional water permeability, which is not the osmotic permeability the form needs
- FL-020: The mapping needs membrane area per bed, which no row holds
- FL-022: Research 14 lists `P_U` per bed class, but only the liver bed's cells make the lumped anion; one site-class row is enough
- FL-024: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the interstitial phosphate amount of a bed at rest.
- FL-025: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the cell and interstitial water of a bed at rest.
- FL-027: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the interstitial magnesium amount of a bed at rest.
- FL-028: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the cell and interstitial water of a bed at rest.

Flags for RN:

- RN-001: The mapping needs the collecting duct's tissue mass, which no row holds.
- RN-001: Binding in blood reads the plasma-water concentration, so V2 sits in the peritubular bed's blood, not at the duct's basolateral side; the occupancy is a stand-in.
- RN-005: Human Kf rests on a modelled glomerular pressure, so the measurement is model-assisted.
- RN-005: The law carries no oncotic rise along the glomerular capillary (RN-L01, Deen-type), so a Kf fitted with that rise does not carry over as is.
- RN-006: `rn.oncotic-slope` and `fl.oncotic-slope` state one physical relation under two keys.
- RN-011: The derivation needs nephron number, segment geometry and tubular fluid viscosity, which no row holds.
- RN-012: The mapping needs the segment's water delivery, a produced quantity; measured distal delivery comes from lithium clearance, which carries its own assumptions.
- RN-014: The mapping needs the segment's water delivery, a produced quantity; measured distal delivery comes from lithium clearance, which carries its own assumptions.
- RN-015: One grouped duct with no medullary gradient (body 01 RN absent) reaches the maximal osmolality only through eta_max, so eta_max binds the observed concentrating limit, not a duct property.
- RN-016: Research 14 row 'Sodium reclaim against pressure and the hormones' writes the capacity as J = J0 f_P (1 + S_II E_AT1) (1 + S_al E_MR) (1 - S_np E_NP), so `rn.sodium-capacity` becomes J0 and its resting value equals the slice's J only where the factors' product is 1 at rest.
- RN-016: Native-contract 10 fits the tubule's sodium capacity to the filtration anchor at initialization, so a sourced value here and that fit would bind one constant twice.
- RN-017: The half-saturation is an amount in the lumen, not a concentration, so any measured K_c binds only through a segment lumen volume that no row holds.
- RN-018: Research 14 row 'Sodium reclaim against pressure and the hormones' writes the capacity as J = J0 f_P (1 + S_II E_AT1) (1 + S_al E_MR) (1 - S_np E_NP), so `rn.sodium-capacity` becomes J0 and its resting value equals the slice's J only where the factors' product is 1 at rest.
- RN-019: The half-saturation is an amount in the lumen, not a concentration, so any measured K_c binds only through a segment lumen volume that no row holds.
- RN-021: A constant research 02's form fixes at 1 is a packet parameter in the crate, so a packet can move the afferent tone at the set point away from 1.
- RN-022: A constant research 02's form fixes at 0.5 is a packet parameter in the crate.
- RN-023: Human TGF is measured only indirectly, so the span binds through the filtration and afferent laws, not alone.
- RN-024: Research 14's renin row reads the same macula densa signal with its own `x0` and `m` (`f_md`), a second set point on one sensed concentration.
- RN-027: The mapping scales a value per 1.73 m² of body surface to the reference man, whose body surface no row holds
- RN-029: The mapping scales a value per 1.73 m² of body surface to the reference man, whose body surface no row holds
- RN-031: Renin is measured as an activity (plasma renin activity, angiotensin I made per volume per hour) or as a direct concentration; the contract's renin amount needs one basis before any renin row maps (research 14, row 'Renin secretion')
- RN-031: The mapping reads resting effects and the resting macula densa concentration, which the engine produces; ticket 05's Comment on the sinus node takes the same route (gains as derivations from resting effects)
- RN-032: `m` is used in research 14 both here (renin's logistic width, a sodium concentration) and in the sodium reclaim row (a pressure width); they need distinct keys (research 14, rows 'Renin secretion' and 'Sodium reclaim')
- RN-033: Two set points now read the macula densa sodium concentration: the slice's `rn.feedback-sodium` for tubuloglomerular feedback and `x0` here for renin; whether they are one constant is a choice ticket 17 should state (research 14, row 'Renin secretion')
- RN-035: Research 02 already uses `S_b` for the sinus node's beta-1 gain; the renin gain needs a distinct key (research 14, row 'Renin secretion')
- RN-036: Research 14 uses `S_A` for two constants, renin's AT1 feedback and aldosterone's AT1 gain; they need distinct keys (rows 'Renin secretion' and 'Aldosterone secretion')
- RN-038: Its part found no row for this receptor's binding constants; the list adds them as RN-082, EN-048, RN-083, EN-049, EN-050.
- RN-039: `J0` restates the slice's `rn.sodium-capacity` with a different definition: the slice's capacity holds at rest with no factors, while J0 is the capacity with all effects at zero (research 14, row 'Sodium reclaim against pressure and the hormones'; `crates/ng-renal/src/shared.rs` line 16)
- RN-040: Pressure natriuresis is a check, never a law (body 01 RN line 390); S_P must not be fitted to it
- RN-042: `m` is reused across research 14's rows (renin's width, RN-034) and research 02 (Katona's multiplier); distinct keys needed
- RN-044: Research 14 uses `S_al` for two constants, aldosterone's gain on sodium reclaim and on potassium secretion; distinct keys needed (rows 'Sodium reclaim' and 'Potassium secretion')
- RN-045: ANP raises filtration too, which no research 14 form carries; only a dose that leaves filtration unchanged maps to S_np
- RN-046: Its part found no row for this receptor's binding constants; the list adds them as RN-082, EN-048, RN-083, EN-049, EN-050.
- RN-047: Its part found no row for this receptor's binding constants; the list adds them as RN-084, EN-051, EN-052.
- RN-048: Its part found no row for this receptor's binding constants; the list adds them as RN-085, EN-053, EN-054.
- RN-049: `J1` and `c_ref` enter only as `J1 c_ref^-n`, so one is a unit choice (research 14, row 'Potassium secretion')
- RN-051: `J1` and `c_ref` are not separately identifiable
- RN-052: Same symbol as sodium reclaim's `S_al` (RN-044) for a different constant
- RN-053: `f_q = 1 + S_q (q / q_ref - 1)` with `J1` leaves three constants for an intercept and a slope, so `q_ref` is a reference choice
- RN-054: No identity takes a flow over a flow to a ratio (`q / q_ref`; research 14, What the contract lacks)
- RN-057: The form puts the whole rhythm on the filtration coefficient, but a measured GFR rhythm also carries the night's fall in pressure and plasma flow, so binding a_g to GFR's amplitude counts those twice
- RN-058: No operator computes a cosine, so `φ_g` cannot be a parameter as written; it becomes cos(φ_g) and sin(φ_g) (research 14, What the contract lacks)
- RN-059: Sulfate's maximum binds T_U only if the lumped anion is counted as sulfate; its charge basis waits on ticket 03
- RN-061: The form puts every ammonium made into urine, while human kidneys return part of what they make to renal venous blood, so J_N0 binds to excretion, not to production
- RN-062: Research 14 uses `G` for the peptide's secretion gain too (EN-028); distinct keys needed
- RN-063: Taking the arterial pH anchor's value as a set point makes the check against that anchor partly circular; ticket 18 should say whether pH0 is a set point (research 14, Decisions, 'Ammonium answers pH with a lag of days')
- RN-064: The rise within 2 h, put down mostly to a more acid urine, is left out of the form
- RN-066: Ganong's threshold is in plasma bicarbonate while the form's c_thr is in total carbon dioxide (research 14, row 'Total carbon dioxide filtered and reclaimed')
- RN-066: Bicarbonate titration expands extracellular volume, which lowers reclaim (Ganong Fig. 39-4, in rats); the form leaves that effect out, so a titration threshold may read low
- RN-077: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the ultrafilterable share of plasma magnesium.
- RN-077: A form you think is wrong: with `c_Mg` the whole plasma concentration ('Rung 0 counts no bound magnesium, so `c_Mg` is the whole plasma concentration', research 15 line 40), the form filters GFR c_Mg where the kidney filters GFR f_uf c_Mg, so T_Mg = T_uf / f_uf keeps the threshold but scales excretion above it by 1 / f_uf.
- RN-077: Research 15 line 40 quotes Rude's maximum 'per 100 mL of glomerular filtrate per 1.73 m²'; a concentration in filtrate takes no body-surface normalization, so the full text must say which quantity it is before the mapping holds.
- RN-082: The mapping needs the tissue mass the site serves, which no row holds.
- RN-082: No part wrote this binding row; the coordinator added it so the `eps` rows RN-038 have the KD they read.
- RN-083: The mapping needs the tissue mass the site serves, which no row holds.
- RN-083: No part wrote this binding row; the coordinator added it so the `eps` rows RN-046 have the KD they read.
- RN-084: The mapping needs the tissue mass the site serves, which no row holds.
- RN-084: No part wrote this binding row; the coordinator added it so the `eps` rows RN-047 have the KD they read.
- RN-085: The mapping needs the tissue mass the site serves, which no row holds.
- RN-085: No part wrote this binding row; the coordinator added it so the `eps` rows RN-048 have the KD they read.

Flags for RS:

- RS-003: The mapping reads FRC, which the curation study lists as anchor RS-A04, so binding `V_L0` from it spends that check.
- RS-003: The mapping needs an end-expiratory oesophageal pressure, which no row holds.
- RS-005: The mapping reads supine and upright FRC, which the curation study lists as anchor RS-A04 and validation RS-V03; ticket 10's run checks only the direction, so binding from FRC spends RS-A04.
- RS-005: The mapping needs a supine end-expiratory oesophageal pressure, which no row holds.
- RS-006: The mapping reads supine and upright FRC, which the curation study lists as anchor RS-A04 and validation RS-V03; ticket 10's run checks only the direction, so binding from FRC spends RS-A04.
- RS-008: The mapping needs the O2 to CO diffusing capacity ratio, which no row holds.
- RS-008: Binding to hemoglobin is instantaneous speciation at rung 0, so the reaction component of Roughton and Forster's split has no home; the form's `DL_O2` must be the whole diffusing capacity, not the membrane component, or transfer runs fast.
- RS-009: The mapping needs the CO2 to O2 tissue diffusion ratio, which no row holds.
- RS-010: Research 12 states saturated vapour pressure as an Antoine form `P_sat(T)` (research 12 line 35); read at core temperature it would make this a produced quantity, while research 11 holds it fixed. RS laws are not among body 01's reference-temperature holds (line 637).
- RS-011: Research 11 line 40 lists `F` among the constants, but it follows from PACO2, FIO2 and R, so holding it constant treats a produced quantity as a parameter (SRS I14).
- RS-013: The curation study lists anatomic dead space as anchor RS-A02; binding `V_D` from the same measurement makes that anchor an identity.
- RS-014: Research 11 line 63 says 'Stolwijk's 44 mmHg and Guyton's 47 mmHg differ in value only'; they name different points, gas saturated at core temperature and gas leaving the mouth cooler, so ticket 05 picks a point, not only a value.
- RS-014: The ideal gas mapping needs the expired gas's temperature, which no row holds.
- RS-015: Research 12's skin evaporation reads a latent heat `lambda` (research 12 line 34); one physical constant should be one record, so the coordinator merges the two rows or names the temperature each is read at.

Flags for BC:

- BC-005: Research 11 line 30 says Table 1 states the standard by red cell pH and the text does not say which pH Eq 9a reads; if it reads red cell pH, the mapping needs red cell pH, which rung 0 holds no space for and can only reach through the red cell pH line (BC-026, BC-027).
- BC-006: Research 11 line 30 says Table 1 states the standard by red cell pH and the text does not say which pH Eq 9a reads; if it reads red cell pH, the mapping needs red cell pH, which rung 0 holds no space for and can only reach through the red cell pH line (BC-026, BC-027).
- BC-007: Research 11 line 30 says Table 1 states the standard by red cell pH and the text does not say which pH Eq 9a reads; if it reads red cell pH, the mapping needs red cell pH, which rung 0 holds no space for and can only reach through the red cell pH line (BC-026, BC-027).
- BC-010: Research 11 line 30 says Table 1 states the standard by red cell pH and the text does not say which pH Eq 9a reads; if it reads red cell pH, the mapping needs red cell pH, which rung 0 holds no space for and can only reach through the red cell pH line (BC-026, BC-027).
- BC-012: Research 11 Decisions (line 58) keep the temperature shift because 'pH, PCO2 and temperature each have a producer at rung 0', while body 01 line 454 holds every speciation at a reference temperature (arrow 94, kept); this part follows body 01 and gives the temperature terms no rows.
- BC-014: Plasma's water fraction follows its protein content, and albumin is an amount at rung 0, so a fixed `W_pl` holds a quantity the body could produce (SRS I14); harmless while albumin moves little, but worth a ruling.
- BC-018: Research 11 states `K1` 'with its pH and temperature terms' (line 33) while research 14 (line 46) takes Antonius's `Kc` as one constant with a unit that does not fit its Eq 9; the two tickets define the record differently until ticket 05 picks one form and one unit.
- BC-019: Research 11 line 33 names 'its pH and temperature terms' without their number or form, so a checker cannot count these rows from the ticket.
- BC-028: Antonius's Table 2 gives albumin as 25 mmol/L, a value that fits only g/L, and the contract's albumin basis (mass or amount) sets a1 and b1's unit; ticket 05 checks the unit before binding (ticket 05 Comment from research 14)
- BC-028: No source on hand states the range of the linear albumin term (research 14, row 'Acidity in every watery space')
- BC-029: Antonius's Table 2 gives albumin as 25 mmol/L, a value that fits only g/L, and the contract's albumin basis (mass or amount) sets a1 and b1's unit; ticket 05 checks the unit before binding (ticket 05 Comment from research 14)
- BC-030: Phosphate is in every watery space but buffering inside cells is out at rung 0; ticket 18 counts or drops phosphate's term in cells (research 14, Interfaces, Research 11)
- BC-032: Antonius's Table 1 gives `Kc` as 10^-6.10 with unit mmol/L while Eq 9 takes `[H+]` in mmol/L; `c_0`, `[H+]` and `K1` must use one unit (ticket 05 Comment from research 14)
- BC-033: The Van Slyke slope of 2.3 is per mole of heme (hemoglobin counted as Hb(Fe)), while research 11's c_Hb counts whole molecules and body 01 makes hemoglobin a mass, so b_Hb is 4 times 2.3 per molecule or the per-heme value on the mass basis (ticket 05 Comment from research 14)
- BC-033: Van Slyke's slope multiplies whole blood hemoglobin, while the BC balance reads concentrations in a blood space; whether c_Hb is per whole blood or per the space's water changes the mapping
- BC-033: Van Slyke's constant term (7.7 mmol/L) is plasma's own non-bicarbonate buffering, which the albumin and phosphate terms already carry, so only the hemoglobin slope maps to b_Hb
- BC-034: `a_Hb` carries red cells' fixed charge because red cells have no space at rung 0, so it is not a property any one measurement binds; it trades off with the lumped anion's admission amount
- BC-035: A form you think is wrong: 'Rung 0 has no globulins (body 01), so their term drops' (research 15 line 36). Bound with Moore's albumin coefficient alone, bound calcium at rest falls short by the globulin term and ionized calcium runs high by it; set to match total bound calcium, `a` is no longer Moore's coefficient. The record must say which.

Flags for EN:

- EN-001: Research 14 row 'Vasopressin secretion and clearance' restates this law: drive d = smooth_max(P_osm - theta, 0, w) / dP with theta = theta0 + k_V (V_cv - V_ref), secretion R1 d with a nightly cosine; it reads osmolality, not sodium, and has no saturating maximum.
- EN-001: Research 14's form has no maximum, so `en.avp-most` has no counterpart there.
- EN-002: Research 14 row 'Vasopressin secretion and clearance' restates this law: drive d = smooth_max(P_osm - theta, 0, w) / dP with theta = theta0 + k_V (V_cv - V_ref), secretion R1 d with a nightly cosine; it reads osmolality, not sodium, and has no saturating maximum.
- EN-002: The slice's set point is a sodium concentration at the logistic's midpoint, research 14's `theta0` an osmolality at the threshold; the two name different points on the curve.
- EN-003: Research 14 row 'Vasopressin secretion and clearance' restates this law: drive d = smooth_max(P_osm - theta, 0, w) / dP with theta = theta0 + k_V (V_cv - V_ref), secretion R1 d with a nightly cosine; it reads osmolality, not sodium, and has no saturating maximum.
- EN-003: Research 14's `dP` is a linear slope in osmolality; the slice's `w` is a logistic width in sodium.
- EN-004: Research 14 keeps first-order clearance 'as in the slice', so this constant carries over; the slice clears the amount at the central veins' route, while a distribution volume spans plasma and interstitium.
- EN-006: If Seike's or Bergman's fit is in plasma insulin concentration per minute rather than secretion, the mapping needs insulin's distribution volume, which no row holds; research 13 does not state the units
- EN-006: Seike fits 46 subjects, only 9 with normal glucose tolerance, so a fit restricted to them or a healthy clamp study binds it
- EN-010: The mapping needs fasting plasma glucagon, which no row holds (it is a reading, not a parameter)
- EN-011: The form reads `G_b`, 'the glucose relative to basal', and basal glucose is a produced quantity (SRS I14); only `G_b x_50` sets the curve, so `G_b` must stay a declared reference and never take the body's own fasting glucose
- EN-012: Text S4 names an effector responsiveness and sensitivity that Table S1 sets for glucose away from 1 for the responsiveness, and research 13 takes both as 1 ('The form takes both as 1, which the basal value of 1.0 supports'); if Schaller's function applies them, Table S2's values are not the form's, so a fit to clamp data should check them
- EN-013: Text S4 names an effector responsiveness and sensitivity that Table S1 sets for glucose away from 1 for the responsiveness, and research 13 takes both as 1 ('The form takes both as 1, which the basal value of 1.0 supports'); if Schaller's function applies them, Table S2's values are not the form's, so a fit to clamp data should check them
- EN-014: Text S4 names an effector responsiveness and sensitivity that Table S1 sets for glucose away from 1 for the responsiveness, and research 13 takes both as 1 ('The form takes both as 1, which the basal value of 1.0 supports'); if Schaller's function applies them, Table S2's values are not the form's, so a fit to clamp data should check them
- EN-015: Text S4 names an effector responsiveness and sensitivity that Table S1 sets for glucose away from 1 for the responsiveness, and research 13 takes both as 1 ('The form takes both as 1, which the basal value of 1.0 supports'); if Schaller's function applies them, Table S2's values are not the form's, so a fit to clamp data should check them
- EN-016: The mapping scales a value per 1.73 m² of body surface to the reference man, whose body surface no row holds
- EN-016: The central veins stand for the kidney and the other tissues together, so the kidney's share folds in here
- EN-017: No measured glucagon clearance is on hand (ticket 05 line 40); Schaller's is a model fit
- EN-017: The scaling from per kg or per m² needs the reference man's body mass or surface, which no row holds
- EN-017: Research 13's form also names a liver clearance 'once its constant is bound', which body 01 line 605 does not keep; a measured whole-body clearance therefore binds `CL_s` whole
- EN-018: The form writes the loss as `k_R c_R`, a concentration times a constant: as an amount rate that makes k_R a volume clearance, while 'first-order clearance' as the slice codes it (`en.avp-clearance`, `relax` on the amount) makes it a rate per min. Ticket 17 must say which (research 14, row 'Renin clearance and angiotensin II')
- EN-019: Renin is measured as an activity (plasma renin activity, angiotensin I made per volume per hour) or as a direct concentration; the contract's renin amount needs one basis before any renin row maps (research 14, row 'Renin secretion')
- EN-019: Angiotensin II is made only in the pulmonary bed's plasma at `k_A c_R V`, so k_A is the whole-body rate divided by that bed's renin amount, not by all plasma renin; the mapping needs the bed's plasma volume, a state no row holds (research 14, row 'Renin clearance and angiotensin II')
- EN-020: A measured clearance includes binding to tissue receptors, which the receptor layer also takes, so binding and clearance could count the same loss twice
- EN-021: Above the threshold `R0` and `dK` enter only as `R0 / dK`, so no source can bind both; one is a unit choice. Quoted: `R = R0 (1 + S_A E_AT1) smooth_max(c_K - K0, 0, w) / dK` (research 14, row 'Aldosterone secretion')
- EN-022: Research 14 uses `S_A` for renin's AT1 feedback too (RN-036); distinct keys needed
- EN-023: Its part found no row for this receptor's binding constants; the list adds them as RN-082, EN-048, RN-083, EN-049, EN-050.
- EN-024: Research 14 does not state Fig. 29-5's species, method or window; check them before binding, because a textbook figure may draw on animal data
- EN-024: The report's own threshold (3.3) and its gain move the zero to about 4.2 to 4.35 mEq/L, so the report's number is not this form's K0 (research 14, row 'Aldosterone secretion')
- EN-025: Above the threshold `R0` and `dK` enter only as `R0 / dK`, so no source can bind both; one is a unit choice. Quoted: `R = R0 (1 + S_A E_AT1) smooth_max(c_K - K0, 0, w) / dK` (research 14, row 'Aldosterone secretion')
- EN-027: Aldosterone's disappearance has more than one phase; a first-order clearance on one amount binds to one of them, and which one should be stated
- EN-028: Arrow 79's atrial wall stress is typed as a pressure (`crates/ng-contract/src/catalog.rs` line 184), but the sources read atrial pressure; if CV states it as wall stress (pressure times radius over wall thickness), the mapping needs atrial radius and wall thickness, which no row holds
- EN-028: The Guyton report reads two atria with the right weighted 2 of 3; one stress term replaces both, so a source's left and right slopes must be combined before binding
- EN-029: Arrow 79's atrial wall stress is typed as a pressure (`crates/ng-contract/src/catalog.rs` line 184), but the sources read atrial pressure; if CV states it as wall stress (pressure times radius over wall thickness), the mapping needs atrial radius and wall thickness, which no row holds
- EN-032: The slice's held vasopressin law (`crates/ng-validate/src/slice.rs` lines 337 to 375) is a logistic of central venous sodium concentration with `en.avp-sodium` (centre), `en.avp-width` and `en.avp-most` (a maximum rate). Research 14 restates the law as a threshold in osmolality with a linear rise, so `θ0`, `dP` and `R1` are defined differently from those keys and cannot reuse their values
- EN-034: No operator computes a cosine, so `φ_n` cannot be a parameter as written; ticket 05 can deliver the clock as cos and sin of its phase and the law takes cos(φ_n) and sin(φ_n) as two parameters (research 14, What the contract lacks)
- EN-035: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the central veins' plasma water, which turns a plateau concentration into a secretion rate under a clearance on the central veins' amount.
- EN-036: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the central veins' plasma water, which turns a plateau concentration into a secretion rate under a clearance on the central veins' amount.
- EN-039: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the central veins' plasma water or their share of the body's PTH, since the form clears PTH only there.
- EN-041: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the clock time of the core temperature minimum, since Scheer counts degrees from it and the contract's phase is zero at midnight (solver contract, boundary port).
- EN-042: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the clock time of the core temperature minimum, since Scheer counts degrees from it and the contract's phase is zero at midnight (solver contract, boundary port).
- EN-043: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the central veins' plasma water or share of the body's cortisol, since the form clears cortisol only there.
- EN-044: No part wrote this binding row; the coordinator added it so the `eps` rows that read this receptor have their KD.
- EN-046: No part wrote this binding row; the coordinator added it so the `eps` rows that read this receptor have their KD.
- EN-048: The mapping needs the tissue mass the site serves, which no row holds.
- EN-048: No part wrote this binding row; the coordinator added it so the `eps` rows EN-023 have the KD they read.
- EN-049: No part wrote this binding row; the coordinator added it so the `eps` rows that read this receptor have their KD.

Flags for HP:

- HP-002: At the liver the crossing acts in series with a first-order reaction (`k_fa` (HP-022)), and splanchnic uptake binds only their combination, so one of the two must be set large by a model choice or split by a measured liver cell concentration
- HP-003: At the liver the crossing acts in series with a first-order reaction (`k_X`, glycerol (HP-018)), and splanchnic uptake binds only their combination, so one of the two must be set large by a model choice or split by a measured liver cell concentration
- HP-004: At the liver the crossing acts in series with a first-order reaction (`k_X`, lactate (HP-016)), and splanchnic uptake binds only their combination, so one of the two must be set large by a model choice or split by a measured liver cell concentration
- HP-005: At the liver the crossing acts in series with a first-order reaction (`k_aa` (HP-020)), and splanchnic uptake binds only their combination, so one of the two must be set large by a model choice or split by a measured liver cell concentration
- HP-007: 13C spectroscopy measures net glycogen change, so synthesis and breakdown bind only jointly; Taylor's split of direct and indirect sources does not separate them
- HP-008: The mapping from a concentration (or a share of liver mass) needs the liver's mass, which no row holds
- HP-010: Its part found no row for this receptor's binding constants; the list adds them as HP-038, MT-077, MT-078, EN-044, EN-045.
- HP-010: Research 15 lowers this effect's efficacy under cortisol (research 13, interfaces), so the healthy value binds at the day's cortisol
- HP-015: Its part found no row for this receptor's binding constants; the list adds them as HP-039, EN-046, EN-047.
- HP-021: The mapping needs hepatic plasma flow and a measured extraction, and no row holds either
- HP-021: Sherwin's hepatic clearance is computed from others' flows and an assumed extraction (research 13), so its `E` is assumed, not measured; a measured extraction (Ferrannini 1983, named by the curation study, not in the store) would replace it
- HP-022: `k_fa` and the liver's fatty acid crossing act in series (see HP-002)
- HP-028: Boron's shares cover glycogen, triglyceride and protein storage and amino acids to glycogen; no source on hand states the share for gluconeogenesis from lactate or glycerol
- HP-029: Sulfur is no element the ledger balances and ticket 03 settles the lumped anion's makeup, so s's unit (mmol or mEq of lumped anion) waits on it (research 14, What the contract lacks, Reaction grants)
- HP-030: A constant two law-form tickets define differently: research 13's glucose output reads insulin's `E_ins` at the liver with one `eps` (ticket 02's form), while this row makes it `eps_I = eps_I0 / (1 + S_F L)`. Insulin's efficacy measured in healthy people at daytime cortisol is eps_I0 / (1 + S_F L_day), not eps_I0, so research 13's insulin row and this one must agree which they bind.
- HP-033: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the free fraction of plasma cortisol, through which a binding constant on free cortisol maps to the total cortisol the receptor reads.
- HP-034: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the free fraction of plasma cortisol, through which a binding constant on free cortisol maps to the total cortisol the receptor reads.
- HP-038: The mapping needs the tissue mass the site serves, which no row holds.
- HP-038: No part wrote this binding row; the coordinator added it so the `eps` rows HP-010 have the KD they read.
- HP-039: The mapping needs the tissue mass the site serves, which no row holds.
- HP-039: No part wrote this binding row; the coordinator added it so the `eps` rows HP-015 have the KD they read.

Flags for GI:

- GI-001: Body 01 calls GI's hold 'a held compliance', while the slice holds the pressure itself; 'The holds rung 0 keeps' says a compliance law would need the abdominal contents' volume, so a held pressure is what rung 0 keeps.
- GI-001: The day's posture changes intra-abdominal pressure, and one held value serves every posture.
- GI-006: The mapping scales a per-segment measurement to one grouped lumen, which needs the small intestine's absorbing length (for a grouped value, the jejunum's and ileum's shares of it), and no row holds it
- GI-006: Fordtran 1965's absolute filtration coefficients (p. 1938) are garbled in the store text and research 13 did not use them, so no reading of `L_f` is on hand
- GI-006: The form's Phi_p sums sigma_s c_s over every plasma solute, but its Constants column lists sigma only for each solute the lumen holds; urea, creatinine, ketones, lactate, fatty acids, glycerol, albumin and the other plasma-only solutes need a sigma or a stated omission (research 13 row 'Water across the small intestine')
- GI-007: Fordtran measures sodium chloride as one salt, so `sigma_Na` (GI-007) and `sigma_Cl` (GI-009) bind from one measurement unless a source splits them; the identity mapping is a model choice
- GI-007: The jejunum's and ileum's values differ (research 13 row 'Water across the small intestine'), and one lumen needs a length-weighted value whose weights no row holds
- GI-008: The jejunum's and ileum's values differ (research 13 row 'Water across the small intestine'), and one lumen needs a length-weighted value whose weights no row holds

- GI-010: The jejunum's and ileum's values differ (research 13 row 'Water across the small intestine'), and one lumen needs a length-weighted value whose weights no row holds
- GI-011: The jejunum's and ileum's values differ (research 13 row 'Water across the small intestine'), and one lumen needs a length-weighted value whose weights no row holds
- GI-012: The mapping scales a per-segment measurement to one grouped lumen, which needs the small intestine's absorbing length (for a grouped value, the jejunum's and ileum's shares of it), and no row holds it
- GI-014: The mapping scales a per-segment measurement to one grouped lumen, which needs the small intestine's absorbing length (for a grouped value, the jejunum's and ileum's shares of it), and no row holds it
- GI-028: A held stand-in: research 13's form replaces it, so its row retires then
- GI-029: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the small intestine's absorptive length or area relative to a perfused segment.
- GI-029: A constant two law-form tickets define differently: research 13's fecal exit (`J_exit,i = r_i smooth_max(J_abs,i, 0, w)` with `r_i = (1 - f_i) / f_i`, research 13 line 39) fixes the absorbed share of what leaves the lumen at research 13's `f_i` for this species, so this row's constants set only how fast the lumen empties and cannot show the load- or store-dependent fraction absorbed that research 15 line 31 to 33, 60 and its Decisions give as the reason for the form.

Flags for HC:

- HC-001: Body 01 line 322 makes hemoglobin's partial volume stand for the red cells' volume, which is `1 / c_Hb,rbc`; if body ticket 03 or 08 binds that partial volume as its own constant, two records define one quantity.
- HC-002: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the gut bed blood's share of the body's hemoglobin.
- HC-002: A form you think is wrong: research 15 line 58 scales `k_R` 'from `k` by the blood's share at that site', calling it 'a fixed share of the red cell mass while blood is mixed'. That share is a produced quantity of the state, which moves with posture and splanchnic pooling, so fixing it inside a constant puts a produced quantity in a parameter (SRS I14) and makes the loss move with gut blood volume.
- HC-004: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the liver's storage iron and the liver bed's interstitial iron at rest.
- HC-006: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the gut bed's interstitial iron at rest.

Flags for MS:

- MS-001: Body 01 places the chance fixture at the composition root ('none: the composition root'), while the slice declares it in MS's held card; the system column says MS only because the key does.
- MS-003: Occupancy at this junction needs acetylcholine's release per firing, clearance and binding constants there, which no research 11 row names; research 02's junction forms state them per junction, and whether r02's rows reach the pump's junction is the coordinator's to check.
- MS-004: The scaling to the whole bed needs skeletal muscle mass, which no row holds
- MS-005: The scaling needs skeletal muscle mass, which no row holds
- MS-008: At steady state the muscle bed's synthesis less breakdown must equal creatinine's draw on muscle protein (`k_crn` P_M), so `S` and `B` there cannot both bind freely; one is a derivation in practice
- MS-009: At steady state the muscle bed's synthesis less breakdown must equal creatinine's draw on muscle protein (`k_crn` P_M), so `S` and `B` there cannot both bind freely; one is a derivation in practice
- MS-010: Whole-body turnover splits between the muscle and liver beds only through each bed's share of protein turnover, which no row holds
- MS-011: Whole-body turnover splits between the muscle and liver beds only through each bed's share of protein turnover, which no row holds
- MS-012: The mapping needs the muscle's body protein, a state whose healthy size no row holds
- MS-015: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the bone bed's interstitial calcium amount; tracer studies read exchange with the whole extracellular fluid.
- MS-016: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the bone bed's interstitial water, against which the exchangeable calcium is partitioned.
- MS-017: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the bone bed's interstitial phosphate amount; tracer studies read exchange with the whole extracellular fluid.
- MS-018: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the bone bed's interstitial water, against which the exchangeable phosphate is partitioned.
- MS-019: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the bone bed's interstitial magnesium amount; tracer studies read exchange with the whole extracellular fluid.
- MS-019: Body 01 gives bone's partner pool to magnesium (MS, line 517) but its species table lists bone bed cells among calcium's and phosphate's spaces and not magnesium's (line 549), as research 15 notes under its mineral table; ticket 03 or ticket 19 settles it before this row holds.
- MS-020: A mapping that needs a quantity no row holds: the bone bed's interstitial water, against which the exchangeable magnesium is partitioned.
- MS-020: Body 01 gives bone's partner pool to magnesium (MS, line 517) but its species table lists bone bed cells among calcium's and phosphate's spaces and not magnesium's (line 549), as research 15 notes under its mineral table; ticket 03 or ticket 19 settles it before this row holds.
- MS-021: The mapping needs the tissue mass the site serves, which no row holds.
- MS-021: No part wrote this binding row; the coordinator added it so the `eps` rows MS-003 have the KD they read.
- MS-022: No part wrote this binding row; the coordinator added it so the `eps` rows that read this receptor have their KD.

#### IM. Immune

No row. Body 01 and the body map declare immunity at rest with zero flux, and research 15's section on coagulation and immunity names no constant, because zero is the contract's literal. A first immune law brings its own rows.

### How the list was made

- Six row workers each read one law-form ticket whole (research 02 with the crates' parameter keys and the native-contract tickets for the slice, then research 11 to 15), body 01's tables for their systems and "The holds rung 0 keeps", ticket 05 and record 0003. Each wrote one row per constant to a fixed schema. A seventh part adds the receptor binding rows no ticket wrote.
- The works come from the law-form tickets, ticket 05's Comments and the curation study's `needs.jsonl` and `coverage.jsonl`, by need id. No worker read a source or a curation fill for a value. Whether the evidence store holds a work came from its catalog, by DOI or title.
- A script merges the parts, numbers the rows, ranks the works and counts the routes. The parts, the script and a checker's report on 30 rows across all 13 systems are kept outside the repo with the run's evidence.
